The team then did not wind down, which is step 3 unbuilt and nothing else: a Claude Code worker's `/exit` does not leave (it sits idle at the prompt), the lead's brief winds down only when members have *exited* and forbids closing an idle member that has no stop time, so the lead logs *out of work, not closing* every ten minutes indefinitely. Step 3 must treat *idle and declared* as finished and let the lead close such a member. **Step 3 landed 2026-09-17 (PR #192):** design §4.9a gained *Finished means declared, not gone* (a member is finished when `out_of_work` is on its record and it is idle, exited or closed) and *The lead runs the stop itself*; `ao team stop` gained `--close` (closes each member that settled with no uncommitted file and no unpushed commit, names any it left open), never types at or kills a lead that is the caller, sends no wrap-up to a finished member, and no longer prints the lead as *still working*; the lead template and `docs/briefs/orchestrator-ao-1.md` carry the round rule (a finished member is never sent to or restarted) and the four-step ending (`ao team stop <team> --close`, the declaration, push the log, `ao close` on its own id). **Seen live 2026-09-17 23:16–23:19Z (ao-grind):** `tdgrind-ao-1` declared `out_of_work` after four items; its lead, woken from `ao wait` by the change, ran `ao team stop ao-grind --close --timeout 90` (the member closed 23:18:37Z, nothing left open), recorded its own `ao progress none --why "Every member finished: …"`, pushed its log and ran `ao close` on its own id (23:19:10Z) — three minutes from declaration to a stopped team, nobody involved. The night before, the same state held two teams up for ten hours, by their leads' own count. **Step 6 landed 2026-09-17 (PR #203, session `tdgrind-ao-1`):** both §4.5a rows. The **out of work** chip is on the card beside the report line and in the Focus header — the words fixed, the `why` on hover (a reason names every entry the session looked at and what gates each, which a card cannot hold), drawn for any session that declared and gone the moment it claims again, which is the record's own rule. It is not a state: the card still reads `idle`. The report row is now drawn for a declaration even when neither channel has anything in it. The **Teams** strip reads *wound down <t> ago* rather than *stopped* when nothing carrying the badge is live and every session that carried it declared — all or nothing, from the records rather than from any count of ledger rows, so a team one member never declared on reads *stopped*, and a definition nothing has ever carried is neither. `ao team list` prints the same word from the same rows, so the page and the CLI cannot disagree about one definition. Note which state the strip surfaces: a worker that declared but has not been stopped still sits `idle`, so its team is live and its group card is the surface until step 3's `ao team stop --close` closes it — the strip is the *after*. The review of that PR found the one thing worth finding: both readers treated `out_of_work` as a `{at, why}` dict on sight, and `view` runs for every session on the grid — so a record left in any other shape by a different build, a bug or a hand repair would have taken down the whole Org page rather than costing one card its chip, which is PR #131's `run_until` *half six* failure again in a new field. Both readers check the shape now, `_age` returns "" for anything it cannot read rather than raising, and the tests feed both a string, a list, a number and three malformed instants. **Step 5 built 2026-09-20 (PR #340, `grinder-ao-1`)** in the package's template, `src/agentorc/briefs/manager.md`: after `ao progress none`, one line on the board, `Due:` today, saying the team ran out of work at `<t>` and what each member looked for, from each record's `why`, before the log is pushed and the manager closes itself. **`docs/briefs/manager-ao-1.md`, the repo's own manager brief, still lacks it**; `docs/briefs/` is the anchor's (its instruction to this team, 2026-09-21; the briefs were renamed by it under TD-076 step 4a), and a brief takes effect at a team start. Step 4 is open: the three guard numbers, chosen against a running fleet.

**Location:** `src/sessionorc/models.py` (`out_of_work` on the record), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (the `progress` RPC's new entry shape), `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao progress none`), `src/agentorc/teamrun.py` (the wind-down calls the existing stop sequence), `src/agentorc/ui/` (the chip and the strip note), `agentorc/briefs/*.md` (each role's test), `docs/briefs/orchestrator-ao-1.md`, design §4.9a / §4.5a / §4.7 / §4.8 / §9 invariant 14

**Why:** every stopper in §6 is a clock or a cap — stop time, run window, usage gate, credential lapse, stall — and none of them answers *is there anything left to do?*; `ao team stop` is a person's command that no condition calls (`teamrun.stop_members` runs only when a caller calls it). What looked like wind-down was three brief paragraphs agreeing with each other: the worker's *stop when your lane is done*, the orchestrator's restart rule keyed on *lane items still open*, and the orchestrator's *stop when every member has exited*. That cascade holds for a fixed lane and breaks for `free-pick`, which is the lane the ao-grind team actually runs: *lane done* never becomes true, the lead's idle rule (idle **with lane items not done**) never matches a worker with no lane items, so an idle free-pick worker matches no rule at all and nothing notices it — and the lead cannot end after members that cannot end. Paul, 2026-09-14: *"Does our design also have a team wind down if it runs out of work to do? e.g. no production system to keep checking, no TDs?"* Those two examples sit either side of the distinction §4.9a turns on: a role that consumes a list ends when the list ends; a role that watches a stream is *waiting* when its source goes quiet, and waiting is not finishing.

**Fix, in order — each step is usable on its own:**

1. **The declaration.** `out_of_work: {at, why}` as its own field on the session record — beside the `progress` entry list, never an entry in it, so the upsert-by-reference rule is untouched — written only by `ao progress none --why` on the ungated `progress` channel, persisted and reloaded with the record, never derived (§9 invariant 14). A `why` is required: a declaration without one is refused, naming the rule. Tests: the field survives a reload; a derived-report tick never writes it; `ao status -v` and `--json` show it.
2. **The role tests, in the briefs.** §4.9a's table written into each `agentorc/briefs/<role>.md` template: what a fixed-lane grinder, a free-pick grinder, a hunter and an orchestrator each have to have searched before they may declare it, and the rule that they declare it *before* exiting rather than just exiting. This is prose, and it is the step that makes the rest mean anything.
3. **The lead's half.** The orchestrator's tick: an out-of-work member is not nudged and not restarted; a member that exited **without** declaring it is still a crash and is still restarted (that split is the whole value of step 1); and when every member is out of work or exited having said so, the lead runs the wind-down. The wind-down is `teamrun`'s existing stop sequence called with a different trigger — wrap up the members, wait, then the lead — never a second code path.
4. **The three guards, whose numbers §4.9a deliberately leaves unset:** the lead re-reads the ledger itself before accepting a **team-wide** wind-down (one cheap second opinion catches every mechanical false negative — a `gh` outage, a moved ledger file, a path that changed under a grep); an exhaustion declared within *n* minutes of a session's start is reported rather than acted on; and a member's own re-check interval before it may declare. Choose the numbers here against a running fleet, write them into the design in the same PR, and say what evidence would change them. The failure to guard against is not a team that runs on too long — it is one that stands down because it looked wrong.
5. **The board line.** The lead's last act before its own exit: one line on `docs/user_attention.md` naming the time and, per member, what it looked for and did not find, taken from each record's `why`. This is the point of the mechanism — the fleet has finished the work a person defined, and the next move is a person's; a team that dissolves quietly is harder to notice than one that says so.
6. **The surfaces** (§4.5a's two rows): the **out of work** chip on the card and Focus header with the `why` on hover, and the Teams strip reading *wound down <t>* rather than a bare zero live count.

**Deliberately not in scope:** restarting a team when work reappears — a fleet that starts itself is a scheduler, which is TD-026's question and a different one; and any core-side count of open ledger rows, which §4.9a rejects by name.

**Related:** design §4.9a (the whole section), §4.9 (the stop sequence this reuses, and `ao team start` already being the restart), §4.8 (the `progress` channel and the role presets that carry the tests), §6 (the clock-and-cap stoppers this is orthogonal to), §9 invariant 14, §10 (the 2026-09-14 entry); TD-032 (a stood-down worker sitting `idle` for 20 h with nothing noticing — the same blind spot from the other side); TD-042 (a brief describes the job, not the run — why the test lives in a repeatable template); TD-052 (mail is how *there is work now* reaches an out-of-work session that is still alive, §4.10's wake budget); TD-026 (the schedule that would start a wound-down team again).


## TD-055: Rename to the glossary's decided words — `lead`, `director`, the `control` grant, `round`, and the retired synonyms

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-16 (raised by Paul)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** build
**Pickable:** no — what is left is `docs/briefs/guardians-orchestrator.md`, Paul's when that team stands up

**Status:** Partly done — the words were decided 2026-09-16 and recorded in `docs/glossary.md` (widened the same day after a Fable review of the whole vocabulary). **Step 1 landed 2026-09-17** (docs and briefs, text only): live `docs/design.md`, `docs/briefs/`, `src/agentorc/briefs/*.md` and `src/agentorc/skill.md` say *lead*, *director* (`docs/briefs/orc-of-orcs.md` → `director.md`), *the host agent*, a lead's *round*, *send*, *policies*/*lead* and *org*/*command session*. Deliberately left for steps 2 and 3, because they are code values the text must keep matching until the code changes: the role value `orchestrator` (`role: orchestrator`, `briefs/orchestrator.md`), the grant `orchestrate`, and `<orc>` in `ao control <orc>` and the card's *under `<orc>`* chip. Also left as written: the UI label **show command runs** (a code string; the §4.5a row must match the control), live session and brief names (step 4), the historical `tdgrind` supervisor script, `nudge_user_attention.py`, prior-art product descriptions in §3, and every dated record (§10, `docs/decisions/`, the archive). **Step 2 landed 2026-09-17**: the preset is `lead` (`repoconfig.PRESETS`, `agentorc/briefs/lead.md`, `org.DEFAULT_LEAD_ROLE`), and `repoconfig.ROLE_ALIASES` keeps `orchestrator` resolving for one release — as `--role`, a team's `role:`, and a `roles:` key in either config layer — with one stderr line per process; a team start records the new name even from an old definition. Design §4.5a/§4.8/§4.9/§4.9a/§5 and the glossary say `lead`; source comments that used *orchestrator* for the position were reworded. Paul's live `~/.agentorc/org.yml` still says `role: orchestrator` and a `roles: orchestrator:` overlay: it keeps working through the alias and prints the line — renaming those keys is his (board). **Step 3 landed 2026-09-17**: the grant is `control` (`models.GRANTS`), with `models.GRANT_ALIASES` reading `orchestrate` for one release — a stored record is normalised on load and rewritten (the host agent logs it), `set_grants` / `create` accept it, `--grant` and `ao grant`/`ao revoke` accept it and print a line, and a `grants:` in `.agentorc.yml` or an `org.yml` team says so once; the gate and the UI check `has_control`, which reads either name, so a UI or CLI newer than the running host agent still recognises its records. `ao control <controller> add|remove`, the card's *under `<controller>`* chip, the no-controller hint, the UI's Grants checkbox and notes, design (§4.5a, §4.7, §4.8, §4.9, §4.10, §5, §9), the skill file and every brief say `control`. **Skew, until the host agent restarts:** a `control` sent by the new client is refused by an agent still running the old code (`unknown grant control`) — `ao team start` of a team with a lead, `ao new --role lead`, `ao grant … control`; restart the host agent before those (board). Step 4 (live session and brief names) is Paul's and not done. ~~**What remains after the alias release:** delete `ROLE_ALIASES` and `GRANT_ALIASES` and their tests.~~ **Done by TD-107 (2026-09-22, PRs #440 and #442)**, which removed the aliases outright rather than after a release — so the *done when* below that asks old words to keep working with a deprecation line is superseded: they are unknown words now (design §4.8 *Role names*). Step 4 is done for the teams that run (TD-076 step 4a, 2026-09-20); **what is left of this entry is the one brief never launched, `docs/briefs/guardians-orchestrator.md`** — its name and its words (it still says *lead* for the manager), Paul's to change when the guardians team is first stood up. **The mockups were renamed 2026-09-20** with TD-074's step 6 redraw: the artboards say `lead` and `control`, in the role badges, the Focus headers and the notes. The session *names* they show (`orchestrator-ao-1`) are step 4's and stay as they are.

**Location:** `src/agentorc/repoconfig.py` (the built-in `orchestrator` role, the commented example), `src/agentorc/org.py` (`DEFAULT_LEAD_ROLE = "orchestrator"` and its example), `src/agentorc/briefs/orchestrator.md`, `src/agentorc/teamrun.py`, `src/agentorc/cli.py` (`ao control`'s positional `orc` and its help text, the `<orc>` in `cmd_control`'s docstring and in the no-controller hint at `cli.py:478`, `--grant`), `src/sessionorc/models.py` (`GRANTS`) and `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (`rpc_set_grants`, `_gate`), `src/agentorc/skill.md`, `src/agentorc/ui/` (the Grants checkbox, the "under `<orc>`" wording, any user-facing "orchestrator"), `tests/`, `docs/design.md` (every live use — not §10 or other dated history), `docs/briefs/`, `CLAUDE.md`

**Why:** "orchestrator" meant a team lead in about 200 places while the top coordinator was "orc-of-orcs"; bare "the agent" meant the host daemon in about 70 places of a design whose ADR calls an AI session an *agent*; *tick* meant both the host agent's loop (in code) and a lead's loop (in briefs); and *nudge*, *supervisor* and *fleet* each gave one thing a second name. Paul, 2026-09-16: *"having the names nailed down will reduce confusion"* and *"make the names as intuitive as possible, and keep a single genre when appropriate."* The glossary's rule: organisation words are workplace words, mechanism words are plain and tool-native, no third genre (doorbell the named exception).

**Fix, in order — each its own PR:**

1. **Docs and briefs** (text only). In `docs/design.md` live text, `docs/briefs/`, `src/agentorc/briefs/*.md`, `src/agentorc/skill.md` and `CLAUDE.md`: *orchestrator* as a position → *lead*; *orc-of-orcs* → *director* (`docs/briefs/orc-of-orcs.md` → `director.md`); bare *the agent* for the daemon → *the host agent*; a lead's *tick* → *round*, while the host agent's stays *tick* (including §4.10's "the agent's next pass" → "the host agent's next tick"); *nudge* → *send*; *supervisor* → *lead*, or *policies* where §6's mechanism is meant (the historical "tdgrind supervisor" script keeps its name); *fleet* → *org* or "every live session"; *command run* as a noun → *command session*; "run N" never written; the lead brief's "your reach is your members" → "your members are …"; §5's `name: orc` example → `name: lead`. Dated history (§10 entries, `docs/decisions/`, the archive, old board lines) is left as written. **Already done 2026-09-16:** §4.10, the §4.5a mail rows, invariants 5, 11 and 13 (invariant 9 checked and needed nothing), and TD-052 (the mail design worked on that day).
2. **The role preset.** `orchestrator` → `lead` in `repoconfig.py`'s built-ins, the brief template file, tests, and any `.agentorc.yml` or team definition that names `role: orchestrator`. Accept `orchestrator` as a deprecated alias for one release, printing a line that names the new word.
3. **The grant.** `orchestrate` → `control` in the grant list, the gate's check, `--grant`, role `grants:`, the UI checkbox and `ao --skill`. Records and configs already carrying `orchestrate` are read as `control` for one release (normalised on load, so the record is rewritten with the new value), with a deprecation line. CLI help and hints stop using `<orc>`: `ao control <controller> add|remove …`.
4. **Live session and brief names** (`orchestrator-ao-1`, `guardians-orchestrator`) — Paul's call, since renaming a live session changes what its members' `controllers` name; do it at the next planned restart, not mid-run.

**Not in scope:** record field names (`controllers`, `capabilities`, `role`, `lane`, …), state names, `ao` verbs, package and command names, and code variable names such as `fleet` — the glossary keeps all of them deliberately.

**Done when** no live text in `docs/design.md`, `src/` or the briefs uses *orchestrator* for a position, bare *agent* for the daemon, *tick* for a lead's loop, or *nudge*/*supervisor*/*fleet* in prose; `ao new --role lead` and `ao grant <id> control` work; and `--role orchestrator`, `orchestrate` in a config and `orchestrate` on an existing record still work with their deprecation line.

**Related:** `docs/glossary.md`; ADR `docs/decisions/2026-09-13-org-teams-projects.md` (the Org/Team/Project/Role vocabulary this extends); design §4.8 (role presets, grants, *Orc-of-orcs is not a special case*), §4.9 (team definitions), §6 (policies), §9 invariants 1, 9 and 11.


## TD-057: Sessions on different hosts cannot talk — build the home and node split (design §4.4a)

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-16 (raised by Paul: *"We also have the case of agents on different machines - will ao solve this?"*)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Pickable:** no — what is left is the *Done when* on Paul's machines

**Status:** Partly done — **the two step-2 rules confirmed by Paul 2026-09-23** (an offline node refuses `ao progress`, `ao finding` and a person's mail rather than keeping them on the replica). **The design landed first, as §4.4a (2026-09-16)**, after a Fable review and Paul's decisions (kmaster is home; offline hosts let a person act but refuse their sessions' mail and acts; the relay out of scope but the link written as its protocol). Replaces phase 2's hub-and-spoke ssh transport item (§7). **Step 1 landed with TD-052 step 1 (PR #163, 2026-09-17)**: `host` on every record (filled at create and adoption, backfilled on load); `naming.split_address` / `naming.qualify` as the one normaliser, applied to `controllers` and `to` on the way in; message and `sends` ids, `at` and an `ask`'s bound minted at the host agent; both gates in `sessionorc.mail` as functions over a record map; the caller's `@host` dropped in `_dispatch` because identity is the channel's; and `models.NODE_OWNED` / `HOME_OWNED` / `IDENTITY` tagging every field, pinned by a test that they partition the record. **Step 2 landed 2026-09-17 (PR #198):** `home:` in `hosts.yml` (`hosts.home_name`, `hosts.is_node`; malformed is no home, never a node by accident); the host agent has a `mode` and a `home`, a `host` RPC reporting them, and `home_reachable()` — true for a home, false for a node until step 3, the one line that step changes; `sessionorc.modes.offline_refusal` is the call-by-call table design §4.4a gained in the same PR (reads and a person's node-owned acts served; a session's acts on others, any session's `create`, home-owned edits, the mailbox and reports refused, naming the home, *refused, not queued*), consulted before the gate and never by a home; `models.apply_home` / `apply_node` overlay exactly one owner's fields and refuse a copy that disagrees on identity; `ao team` and the Org strip on a node say the org lives on the home; `ao status` on a node prints the *offline* line on stderr. Two decisions the design had not made are written there: reports are refused offline rather than kept on the replica, and a person's mail is refused too. **Left for step 4, all four since built (checked against the code 2026-09-23, `grinder-ao-1`):** `_derive` returns before writing reports on a node whose link is down; the Org page's `node_banner` says *offline*; the renamed-grant marker went with the aliases (TD-107); a node's team Start and Stop answer 409 with `node_org_note`, the strip's own words. As first written: the tick's *derived* reports still write the home-owned `progress` field on a node; the Org page has no *offline* banner yet; `_overlay` drops `from_dict`'s renamed-grant marker, so a copy carrying an old grant name is normalised without the resave-and-log the loader does; and the Org strip's *the org lives on the home* note never reaches the Start/Stop toast. The review of the PR also found that `sends`, `seen_at` and `wake_refilled_at` are written on an offline node although home-owned: both merges now union them, which the design says. **Step 3a landed 2026-09-17 (PR #200) — the link itself:** design §4.4a gained *The link's protocol*; `sessionorc.link` holds `Mux` (JSON lines, `id`/`re` frames, concurrent requests both ways, `LINK_SILENCE` ends a dead link), `bridge` (`agentorc-agent link --host <name>`, the forced command: announces the key's host to the home's socket, then copies lines) and `dial` (the node's loop: run the transport, `hello`, ping every `LINK_PING`, backoff 1→60 s with jitter, the reason for being down kept in words — *ssh failed* / *agent down on the home* / *refused: …*); `hosts.yml` gained `nodes:` on the home and `link: {ssh|command}` on a node; the home refuses a link from an unauthorised host, from its own name, from a node whose own name disagrees with the key's, from another protocol, and a node refuses every link; a second link from one host replaces the first; `home_reachable()` is the link's state, and the step 2 table **still holds when it is up**, saying *not forwarded yet* rather than *unreachable*. Tested end to end with a home in its own process: up, down when the home stops (*agent down*), up again by itself. **Step 4a landed 2026-09-17 (PR #213, Fable) — acts across the link, one graph, a team on another host:** design §4.4a gained *Acts across the link*; the `act` link method (home → node: `send`, `keys`, `kill`, `close`, `remove`, `decide`, `create`, `name_check`, run through the node's own handler with no gate and no offline table; the reply carries the record, applied at the home before the caller is answered); `_gate` over one graph (`_graph()`: this host's records bare, every other host's as `id@host`, `controllers` re-addressed — `self.sessions` never widened); `create` and `name_check` take `host`, `ao new --host`; the `host_dir` RPC and `stat` link method; `host:` on a team definition, `teams.plan` landing every member there, `teamrun.start` asking the node for each checkout first and refusing the whole team while its host is unreachable, `stop_members` degrading per member; `/term/<id@host>`, `ao tail` and `ao explain` on a remote record refused by name. Tested with the home in its own process: a person's `send` and a lead's `kill` on `id@laptop` run on the node with the verdict back; a caller without `control` refused at the home with the node's handler never called; an act on a dropped link refused *unreachable … not queued* and never run when the link returns; a `create` with `host` landing there, adopted, addressed in the reply. **Decisions the step text did not make:** (1) home-owned edits (`set_controllers`, `set_grants`, `set_stop`, `set_mode`) are applied at the *home's* copy — it owns the fields — but only after the node took the same edit into its replica in the same call, and are refused on an unreachable host like any act, so a node's stopping policies never read an intent the home does not hold; 4b's general push on reconnect supersedes the per-call push. (2) Addresses cross in the reader's form: the home rewrites `controllers` and the `caller` into the node's form on the way out and reads a remote record's `controllers` back into its own, on the card too; a home edit on a remote record is stored in the node's form. (3) The existence check is a `stat` link method behind a `host_dir` RPC, not a dry-run `create`. (4) Roles and briefs are read from the checkout's path on the host running the start (a container node shares it); a machine node without a clone at the same path is refused naming that, until a link method reads them there (4b). (5) `send` without `wait` returns nothing across the link as it does locally. (6) `remove` across the link forgets the record at both ends from the reply. **Read after merge (the anchor's conformance read, 2026-09-18):** `host_dir` and `name_check` with a `host` cross the link for any session with no grant — accepted as the existence-only reads they are (a `stat`, and the name verdict `create` would give), like `occupancy` on one host; the node-side *not my host* refusal gained a direct test with 3c.4. **Not in 4a, still refused:** mail to `id@host` (step 5, as *no session*); the mail gates still read `self.sessions`; the per-record `wait` forwarded from a node, permission prompts answered over the link, a node's own `ao` forwarding to the home, the policy split and the hook spool (4b). **A promote left a linked container node behind (found live 2026-09-18, PR #225, Fable):** contractmatch's node, provisioned 2026-09-17 23:04, was still linked on that build after the promotes of 4a, 5, #219 and 4b — the link's protocol number never changed, the supervisor re-provisioned only on a protocol refusal, and `ao host up` installed the new wheel beside a running agent without restarting it — so every `act`, `forward`, `intent` and `read` would have answered *unknown link method*. Fixed: a build is the first 12 hex of the wheel's sha256 (`containers.build_id`; the wheel is `agentorc-0.0.1-…` at every promote, and `provision` now copies by content, not size); the agent is started with `AGENTORC_BUILD` and an `agent.build` file beside its pidfile; its `hello` carries `build`, kept on the link state; a container node whose build is not `home_build()` is marked `stale` and the supervisor — which now also looks at a linked-but-stale node — re-provisions and restarts it; `host_up` restarts an agent that is behind. A machine node's build is recorded only. Tests: the content-named build and the restart through the fake; the tick re-provisioning a stale linked node and leaving a level one alone; the `hello`'s build end to end. **Verified live at the promote of #225 (2026-09-18 11:45):** the home logged *the node runs build unknown*, the supervisor re-provisioned once, the link dropped and returned, and `ao host status` showed the home's build with no *behind* — no loop. The same promote showed one window, closed in PR #227: `ao service install` wrote the wheel **after** restarting the units, so the restarted agent named the previous build for a moment and a tick landing there could have provisioned from it (self-healing — the per-tick check would have caught it — but an extra restart of the node's agent); the wheel is now written before the restart. **And that order showed the next one, at the promote of #227 (11:54): the old home process, asking on its tick, saw the new wheel a second before systemd stopped it and began a re-provision it could not finish — a `docker exec pip` left running by the stop, no outcome logged, and the new process installing again 30 s later. Closed in PR #228: a linked node that is merely behind waits one `SUPERVISE_GRACE` before the supervisor acts; a node whose link is down is still looked at at once, a link that drops during that grace included (the review's finding). **Left open, small:** the stop leaves an in-flight `docker exec` running — `Runner.cancel()` terminates the docker client, not the process inside the container, and the unit is `KillMode=process`; the grace keeps a promote from starting one, but a stop that lands mid-install for any other reason still orphans it. A fix is to record the container-side pid of an install and kill it from `_stop_supervising`.** **Step 5 landed 2026-09-18 (PR #217, Fable) — mail across hosts:** design §4.4a gained *Mail across hosts*; a node forwards what its table refuses, and every `wait`, to the home as `forward {rpc, params, caller, token}` while the link is up (`_forward`), refused as unreachable while down; the home serves it through its own dispatch as `id@node` (`_forwarded`, `_dispatch(link_host=)`), params read from the node's point of view, the reply's addresses rewritten back (`naming.readdress` over `ADDRESS_KEYS`), `cancel` by token ending a forwarded `wait`; the mailbox, bounds, marks, sweep, reports and the wake decision read one graph of real records (`_graph()` no longer copies; the gates take `controllers=` for the re-addressed view, `_ctl`) and save through `_save` by host; `unreachable` on a `msg` reply and *landed — host unreachable* on `ao msg`. Tested with the home in its own process: a grinder on the node mails its lead at the home, a forwarded `wait` wakes on the lead's reply with the sender in the node's form, the forwarded `inbox` marks it read, the grinder's act on the lead is refused for each half of the gate and runs once granted, a person at the node reaches the org's person inbox, mail during the lid-closed spell lands at the home and is read on return; in-process: the forwarded wait registered under `id@node` and cancelled by token at both ends. **Decisions the step text did not make:** (1) on a node the unread line rides only the replies the home answered — a read the node serves alone has no inbox to count; 4b pushes the count with the home-owned fields. (2) `wait` from a node is always forwarded while the link is up, a person's too. (3) The doorbell across hosts is the forwarded `wait` itself; a hook-confirmed idle on a node rings nothing across the link until 4b. (4) A `create` forwarded from a node session lands on that node unless it names a host. **The laptop test on Paul's machines** (sleep mid-thread, rung once on return) is a live check on the attention board. **Step 3c.5 landed 2026-09-17 (PR #216, Fable) — reach for a container node:** on `hello` from a `container:` node the home runs one docker look off the link (`containers.observe_reach`: container id, name, the node's user) and keeps it as `host_link.reach` on the link state, so every card of the node carries it; `/term/<id@node>` and `ao focus <id@node>` run `containers.attach_argv_in` — `docker exec -u <user> -it <container>` in front of the very `tmux attach` a local session gets, on the bare id, the scroll commands the same way; the card's VS Code link is *attach to running container* by docker's name (`vscode://vscode-remote/attached-container+hex({"containerName": "/<name>"})`, the scheme a browser hands to VS Code — the review caught the bare internal form), replacing the `dev-container+` form 3c.2 printed, which would have opened the person's own container; a machine node's session, or a container node whose look failed, is refused *no terminal reaches it from here*. Tests: `reach`/`observe_reach`/`attach_argv_in` through the fake; `_note_reach` on the in-process agent; the Focus terminal's argv and the card's link with a fake pty; `ao focus --json` on both kinds of node. Live check on the attention board (7). **Step 3c is complete**; 3c.4's `volatile` gap closed with it. **Step 3c.4 landed 2026-09-17 (PR #215, Fable) — occupancy across the home and its container nodes:** `occupants()` at the home reads every container node's records over the directory beside its own (`id@host (state)`) while that node's link is up (down is a blip or a stopped container, whose sessions are dead — review of PR #215), so `create` here, the `occupancy` RPC and the New session form see a session in the container holding the checkout; a create routed to a container node is checked at the home first, over the same set, then by the node over its own (`_check_occupancy_for`); a machine node's records are never read for it; the worktree path is `gitinfo.worktree_path`, factored out of `ensure_worktree` so the check resolves it as `create` does (review). **Also here, the `volatile: true` gap 3c.3 left:** `containers.decide` takes `volatile`, and for a volatile node a container that is not running is *left as the person left it* (a `wait` with that on the card) while an agent missing inside a running one is still started; `decide` now always returns `(action, doing)`, `wait` included. Tests: the decision table's volatile rows and a tick with a stopped volatile container issuing no `up`; the home refusing a create while the container's session holds the checkout, and the reverse refused before the link, a worktree and a machine node not held. Live check (after the promote and the person's steps): `ao new` in `~/contractmatch` on kmaster while a session inside holds it, and `ao new --host contractmatch -d ~/contractmatch` while one here does. Next: 3c.5 reach. **Step 3c.3 landed 2026-09-17 (PR #212, Fable) — the supervisor:** in the home's tick, for each `container:` node whose link is down, `containers.decide` over (container state, agent pid alive, the link's reason) — gone/stopped → the whole idempotent `host_up`; running with no agent → start it; a `hello` refused for protocol (now recorded on the link state for an authorised node) → re-provision and restart; running and alive with the link merely not up → wait the grace — acted on in its own task with backoff (5 s doubling to 5 min), and the card's overlay (`host_link.supervisor.doing`, drawn as the pill's title and a row) naming which. The suite gained a guard: `containers.Runner` refuses under `pdm run test`, after the tick test's first run reached the real devcontainer CLI. Live check waits on the person's steps below. **Step 3c.2 landed 2026-09-17 (PR #211, Fable) — `ao host up|rebuild|forget|status` and `sessionorc/containers.py`:** the base built by `devcontainer build` from the repo's image keys (tagged `agentorc-node-<name>-base`) and the node on a generated Dockerfile stage that adds tmux — not a local feature, which the CLI takes only from the repo's own `.devcontainer/` (found live); the person's mounts stood in by tmpfs, the checkout at the same path, `--id-label dev.agentorc.node=<name>`; refusals by name (CLI, wheel, Python 3.12+, uid, tmux, a lifecycle command that died); the wheel onto the volume, venv, `hosts.yml`/`profiles.yml` for the node, `gh auth setup-git` with an `env` file; `docker exec -d` with a pidfile; `forget` edits `hosts.yml` by lines and asks the home to close the host's records (`forget_host`, a person's act); `ao service install` writes the wheel. **Live on kmaster 2026-09-17, from a scratch home (the live install is not yet promoted):** the base built (4.6 GB, kept as `agentorc-node-contractmatch-base` for the real run — `docker rmi` it if the disk needs the room), tmux installed, the container came up, the agent inside dialed the mounted socket and the home's card read *linked*; `forget` removed it. contractmatch's `postCreate.sh` failed on its hardcoded `/workspaces/contractmatch` — the person's re-point step, now proven necessary, and `up` names it. Next: 3c.3 the supervisor. **Step 3c.1 landed 2026-09-17 (PR #210, Fable) — the per-node link socket:** the home binds `~/.agentorc/links/<name>/link.sock` for every `nodes:` entry at start (directory `0700`, socket `0600`, re-bound on every start, so a container is handed the directory), a connection on it entering `_serve_link` with the configured name and no bridge; a node's `link: {socket: <path>}` opens it directly, the dialer's socket transport sharing the hello-and-ping conversation with the ssh one and saying *cannot connect* where ssh says *ssh failed*. Tested with the home in its own process: up with no ssh, down and back through a restart of the home. `ao host up` and `containers.py` are 3c.2, next; step 4a's brief, `docs/briefs/td057-step4a.md`, was run by the anchor session itself and removed with the 4a PR. **Step 3b landed 2026-09-17 (PR #202) — a node's records at the home:** design §4.4a gained *A node's records at the home*; a node sends `snapshot` after `hello` and, once it is acknowledged, `report` (at once for `state`/`pending`/`exit_code`/`pane`, else at most every `REPORT_EVERY` 5 s per record) and `gone`; the home takes only records whose `host` is the name the link's key is bound to, adopts an unknown one whole, applies `apply_node` to a known one, and treats a snapshot as the truth about which sessions that host has; another host's records live in `self.remote` and `remote/<host>/`, **apart from `self.sessions`**, so the home's tick, anchor rule, name checks and `create` never meet one; `list`, `get`, `subscribe` and `wait` carry them with `id` = `id@host`; while the link is down their view reads `unreachable` with `host_link` and `last_state` — an overlay, the record untouched — and a restarted home shows them so until the node dials in; an act on `id@host` is refused by name (*step 4*); the card shows the record's own host and no VS Code link. **Test, as step 3 asked:** a home in its own process, the lid closes (the link drops, the node's session lives on), the card goes `unreachable` with its last state kept, comes back by itself on the reconnect's snapshot, and a later change on the node reaches the home. **Not in 3b, for step 4:** the gates and mail still read `self.sessions` only, so another host's record is in no graph yet; the Focus terminal of a remote session has nothing to attach to; a node's `vscode_host` is not known to the home; `/term/<id@host>` fails cleanly only because tmux's exact-name match finds no such session — it wants an explicit refusal; and `teamrun.stop_members` would abort on the first remote member's refusal rather than degrade per member, unreachable until a team spans hosts. Steps 4–5 stay open. **Step 4b.1 landed 2026-09-18 (PR #222) — a node's pane read across the link, and a permission answered over it:** `tail` and `explain` on `id@host` go to that node as reads, ungated, refused *unreachable … not queued* while the link is down, the reply readdressed and otherwise untouched; `ao tail`/`ao explain` then work on a node's session and say *unreachable* in one line. A permission waiting on a node is answered at the home (end-to-end test: the hook blocks on the node, the person decides on `id@laptop` at the home, the hook returns that answer); with the link down the card reads *host unreachable — answer it at <host>*, `decide` is refused and the hook blocks to its own timeout. **Decisions the brief left:** (1) the reads are their own set, `NODE_READS`, crossing as their own link method `read` whose allowlist is that set alone — not added to `NODE_ACTS`, so a read can never reach an acting method through an allowlist and `act`'s never grows by a read; no caller crosses with a read. (2) The mark is `host_unreachable: true` on the view's `pending`, an overlay beside `unreachable` — the record keeps its pending — and the card and Focus show no Allow/Deny under it. (3) A node serves reads of its own panes only: `ao tail <home session>@kmaster` at a node is *no session*, not forwarded (added to PR #219's person-bound test). (4) The machine node's terminal refusal no longer names step 4b: the terminal over the link is *Later*. **Step 4b.2 landed 2026-09-18 (PR #223) — the home's intent reaches the replica, the node's own arrivals reach the home:** the `intent` link method pushes each node its records' home-owned policy fields and unread count, once after the snapshot and then as they change, and the node takes them with `apply_home`; its tick's stopping policies are proven offline end to end (wrapped up and killed with the link down; on reconnect the home shows the node's `exited` and `wrapup_sent_at` beside its own `run_until`); a node's derived reports go to the home and none are derived offline; a person's offline create is adopted with its `controllers`, and a closed home record of the same id is superseded by it. **Decisions the brief left:** (1) 4a's per-call push for the four home edits **stays** — it is what makes an edit on an unreachable host a refusal rather than a divergence; the general push is additive and repairs what still drifts (a home restored from an old store, a routed edit whose second half failed). (2) The pushed fields are `HOME_OWNED` less the mailbox (`inbox`, `outbox`, `threads`, `wakes`, `mail_decided`), less `sends` (every send runs on the pane's own node first, and the merge unions it) and less `superseded_by` (the node's own resume writes it and a report never carries it — the home's copy would clobber it); a pushed `run_until` change resets `wrapup_sent_at` as `set_stop` does; `wake_budget_spent` rides beside `unread` because the mail line carries both. (3) Derived reports cross as their own link method `derived {id, progress, findings, retire}`, not a forwarded `progress`: a derived entry carries the branch it came from and a retire, which the RPC cannot say; a `declared` entry through it is refused whole. (4) The supersede replaces the closed record in place under its id — §4.1's rule for a new session of a finished one's name — so the old one's mail goes with it, as on one host; its run log stays on its node. (5) **The spool of hook events is not built, by decision**: nothing at the home consumes a node's hook events (`_apply_event` runs on the session's own host, `hook` is never forwarded), so §4.4a now says the snapshot of records is the whole of it; a future consumer of events at the home re-opens this. **Step 4b.3 landed 2026-09-18 (PR #224) — a team on a machine node, the nightly tarball, a node's own UI; step 4 is complete:** a team whose checkout is not a directory on the host running the start (a machine node) has its `.agentorc.yml` and briefs read on that node — the home's `host_files` RPC over the `files` link method, `read_checkout` confining every path to the checkout — through `repoconfig.load_text` and a brief read taking a reader, so `teams._launch` has one path; the home writes `backups/store-<date>.tar.gz` once a day; a node's Org page says whether its link is up, its Teams strip and Start/Stop toast say the org lives on the home, and a copy crossing the link with a renamed grant is saved and logged. **Decisions the brief left:** (1) The link method is generic — `files {dir, paths}`, the text of files inside a checkout — so `sessionorc` still never names `.agentorc.yml`; bounded at 16 paths of 256 KiB per call, the directory a git checkout, regular files only judged and read through one descriptor, symlinks followed and refused if they land outside, and a brief the repo keeps outside its checkout refused before it is asked for. (2) `host_files` is a person's read or a `control` holder's — either may already start a session in any directory of a linked host, so it grants neither anything new — not ungated like `host_dir`: it returns file contents, not existence. (3) It is **never served to a call forwarded from a node**, a session's included (`modes.HOME_ONLY`): without that a compromised laptop could forward `host_files` as one of its sessions and read any host's files through the home — `dir` itself is the home's configuration, not confined. (4) The tarball is taken at the first tick of each local date and skipped when that day's file exists, so a restart does not take a second; members are walked as regular files only (no symlink, no socket), written `0600` under a temporary name and renamed. (5) On a node the Org page tolerates the refused mailbox (the person inbox count reads 0 under the offline line) rather than failing whole — it failed before this. (6) The machine node's terminal and a node showing the org stay not built, the second written into §4.4a as decided. **What is left of TD-057 is its *Done when* on Paul's machines** (the live checks on the attention board). **Security read of step 5 (anchor, 2026-09-18, PR #219):** a person's request forwarded from a node was not held to that node's records — `id@<home>` collapsed to a home record and the person gate passed it, so a person at any linked laptop could have set any session's `controllers` or `grants`; now refused at the home (`modes.PERSON_NODE_BOUND`, checked in `_forwarded`), with a test that forges it. Not exploitable before the fix: no node was linked. **Found 2026-09-21 (grinder-ao-1, reading TD-075's `--keep-mail`), not built:** a supersession on a **node** never reaches the home's mailbox. The mailbox is the home's (`inbox`, `outbox`, `threads`, `wakes`, `mail_decided` are left out of `INTENT_FIELDS`), and `superseded_by` is written by the node and, per the comment above `INTENT_FIELDS`, *the home does not hear*. So a resume on a node (`_supersede` → `_move_mail` there) moves an empty mailbox, and so does `ao new --keep-mail` (`create` → `_move_mail` on the node): the old record's mail stays on the home's copy of the old record, and the home's `_msg` forwarding walk, which reads `superseded_by`, does not forward new mail to the successor either. A techlead seat on a node would therefore forget exactly the questions that caused its fill (design §4.9b). Fix direction: the node's report carries the supersession (old id → new id, and whether mail moves), and the home runs `_move_mail` on its own copies and records `superseded_by` there; a test in `tests/test_link.py` with `node_agent` — a resume and a `keep_mail` create on the node, then the home's inbox of the successor. **Built 2026-09-21 (grinder-ao-1, PR #371; `src/sessionorc`, for the anchor to merge), and wider than found:** a name taken again on a node replaces the record **in place**, and the home merged that report into the old record by owner, so the new session also came up at the home with the old run's home-owned fields: its `run_until`, `out_of_work`, `doing`, `progress` and its mail, which a fresh start is meant to drop. That was pushed back to the node as intent, so a team restarted on a node would have read its members as already out of work. Now `supersedes` (`{id, mail, at}`, node-owned, written by `create` and `_supersede`) says what a create replaced or continued. The home takes it once (`_take_supersession`, from the report or the routed create's reply). A same-id replacement is taken whole; the mail moves when `mail` is set and otherwise goes; a resume under another id moves the mail and records `superseded_by` as the graph addresses the successor. `_move_mail` and `_attention_gone` now read addresses, so they work on a node's records at the home. Tests: `test_a_fresh_start_on_a_node_is_a_new_record_at_the_home_and_keep_mail_keeps_the_mail` (home and node, end to end) and `test_a_resume_on_a_node_moves_the_homes_mail_and_forwards_what_is_still_addressed_to_it`. Both fail without the change. Design §4.4a: *A supersession on a node is done again at the home*. **The anchor's read of #371 (fixed in it):** taking a successor whole dropped the home's `suspended` mark on a node's word (suspend while the link is down → the name retaken on the node → the link back). The mark now stands on the successor. A person's create through the home lifts it (`_lift_by_person`). `test_a_suspension_outlives_a_supersession_on_a_node_and_a_persons_create_at_the_home_lifts_it` covers both halves, driven from the node's end. **Design review 2026-09-22:** the container node is built and working for one container and should not be extended further — the next container anywhere should be a plain ssh node provisioned by hand until a second devcontainer project exists; the generated definition, the wheel provisioning and the supervisor stay as they are.

**Location:** `src/sessionorc/hosts.py` (`home:`), `src/sessionorc/models.py` (`host` on the record), `src/sessionorc/naming.py` (the `id@host` normaliser), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (home and node modes; `_gate` and the message gate as functions over a record map; routing acts to nodes), a new link module (`agentorc-agent link`, multiplexed JSON lines over a byte pipe — ssh, or a per-node link socket for a container node), `src/sessionorc/store.py`, `src/agentorc/ui/` (talks to the home), design §4.4a, §4.5b, §4.6, §5, §7, §9 invariant 15

**Why:** each host agent held only its own host's records and phase 2 connected hosts hub-and-spoke through the UI host, so a session on one host could be neither gated for an act nor mailed by a session on another. The gap was a session graph spanning hosts, not a route (§4.4a).

**Fix, in order — each its own PR:**

1. **Addresses** — may land with TD-052 step 1, single-host: `host` on every record; one normaliser that qualifies ids on the way in, storing `id@host` only for another host's session; message ids, `at` and client nonces minted by the host agent, never the CLI; the gate and the message gate written as functions over a record map rather than methods reaching into `self`; the caller's host assigned from the channel a request arrived on, never from a field; and the record's fields tagged node-owned or home-owned as §4.4a lists them. After this, nothing stored needs migrating when a second host arrives.
2. **Home and node modes**: a top-level `home:` in `hosts.yml`; an agent with no `home:` (or naming itself) is home, exactly as today; a node keeps tmux, pty, hooks, pane classification, run logs, git status and usage, and **keeps its own host's records on disk as a replica** whose home-owned fields the home overrides. `org.yml` lives on the home.
3. **The link**: `agentorc-agent link --host <name>` as an ssh forced command in the home's `authorized_keys`, the host name taken from that line and never from the node, the home's `hosts.yml` the list of authorised nodes; node-initiated, JSON lines multiplexed by request id, backoff reconnect, `unreachable` diagnosed as §4.6; the home accepts from a node only reports about, and routes acts only to, records on that node's host. Test: laptop closed for an hour, its cards go `unreachable` and come back.

   **Amended 2026-09-17 (Paul: contractmatch cannot be ground by a worker on kmaster, because the Flutter SDK is in its devcontainer and not on the host; *the grinder can wait*, and then: *the best long-term and robust design, regardless of extra work*). Two Fable reviews the same day: the first found the first container question is the path, not the link; the second, taking the design over, found the link socket must be mounted as a directory, that both ends of the link need code, and — on Paul's question whether the person should install `ao` in the container — that the home should provision the container itself.** The mechanism is design §4.4a *A container node*; this is what was learned and the build list, as **step 3c**.

   **The checkout must be mounted at the same absolute path inside the container.** contractmatch's devcontainer declares no `workspaceMount`, so the checkout is `/home/kmaster/contractmatch` outside and `/workspaces/contractmatch` inside (the Dockerfile's `WORKDIR`, `setup_scripts/postCreate.sh`, the repo's `.claude/settings.json` and `workers-api/assets/gen_og.py` all hardcode the inside path). Two things the earlier drafts parked as design questions are consequences of that and of nothing else:
   - **git worktrees break across the boundary.** `git worktree add` writes absolute paths both ways, and a team puts its lead and every grinder in a worktree. A worktree made inside points at `/workspaces/…` and is broken on the host; worse, `git worktree prune` on the host — which the cadence's own sweep runs — deletes the container's worktree metadata *while a grinder is in it*.
   - **Invariant 2's "identity that survives a mount namespace" is mostly just the path.** `occupants()` compares `Path(dir).resolve()`, and it never reads `repos.txt` (that registry feeds the New-session list and dev-cadence's `/attention`), so "register the repo on one host" — an earlier draft — was wrong in mechanism. Mount the checkout at the identical path and the home can check occupancy over its own records and the container's, because it knows from the `container:` entry that the paths are the same directory. What no agent can see is the person's own `claude` in the person's own VS Code container: `external_sessions()` reads a profile's config dir and tests liveness by pid, which means nothing across pid namespaces. That case is the person's, as a terminal on another machine is today, and the design says so.
   The home's generated definition sets `workspaceMount` and `workspaceFolder` (§4.4a); the repo's part is re-pointing the four files above (and the `safe.directory` lines the Dockerfile and `postCreate.sh` bake for the old path — harmless today only because the uids match) and pinning the user (`useradd --uid 1000`: Debian hands `developer` 1000 today because it is the image's first user, which is luck, not a contract).

   **The link. A container is a node like any other and dials out; `docker exec` is backwards for it** — `link:` is read only by a node dialling out (`_dial_home`, gated on `mode == "node"`) and `bridge()` connects to `paths.socket_path()`, its own namespace's socket, so the command would re-enter the same container and connect to itself: a loop, not a link. (True of *the link only*: `docker exec` is the right verb host→container, and it is how the terminal reaches a container.) The shape is **a per-node link socket at the home**: `serve` binds, for each `nodes:` entry, `~/.agentorc/links/<name>/link.sock` (`0600`, its directory `0700`), speaking only the link protocol, and a connection on it *is* that node, so `_serve_link` runs with the name from the home's config and never from the node's argv — §4.4a's rule kept, with no sshd in the image, no key, no network, and §4.5b intact. **The container gets the directory bind-mounted, never the socket file**: `serve` unlinks and re-binds its socket on every start (`agent.py`, `serve`), a bind mount of a file keeps the old inode, so a mounted socket file is dead after the first restart of the home — which every TD-062 promote is. Code at **both** ends: on the home, the listeners in `serve` and a connection handler that enters `_serve_link` with the configured name; on the node, `link: {socket: <path>}` in `hosts.py` and a branch in `link._dial_once` that opens a unix connection instead of a subprocess, with *ssh failed* worded *cannot connect*. Two shapes considered and not taken: `link: {ssh: <home>}` from inside the container (zero code, but an sshd reachable from a bridge network and a key in the image — the shape for a container on *another* machine, which is a plain ssh node); and ~~the home's whole `~/.agentorc` bind-mounted in~~, **struck** — it hands the container the home's `agent.sock`, where an unqualified caller is *a person at the home*: kill any session, `create`, set grants, plus the store, run logs and the person inbox. Not a weaker trust boundary — no boundary.

   **Who installs the agent — the home does (Paul's question, 2026-09-17).** Not the person by hand (`pipx install agentorc` inside drifts from the home's version until the link refuses it, and dies with the next image rebuild) and not the repo's Dockerfile (couples the project's devcontainer to agentorc and rebuilds the image at every promote). The home generates the node's devcontainer definition from the repo's — `build` paths re-anchored to absolute, since the file lives under `~/.agentorc/nodes/<name>/` — with the person's `mounts`, `customizations` and `containerEnv` dropped — contractmatch's `/mounted/stuff_for_containers_home` carries `.netrc`, `.kube` and `.modal.toml`, the person's whole credential set (Sonnet review, 2026-09-17: the earlier draft saw only `.netrc`) — and each dropped mount replaced by an empty tmpfs at its target, because a repo's lifecycle script may assume the path (contractmatch's `postCreate.sh` runs `mkdir -p /mounted/dev/…` under `set -e`, second Sonnet round: it would have died there), brings it up under its own id label as a second container from the project's image, installs its own wheel into a venv on the node's volume, writes the node's `hosts.yml`, and supervises the agent from its tick. The node's `AGENTORC_HOME` is on that volume: lose it on a rebuild and the run logs (invariant 3) and the waits go with it, and the node reconnects with an empty snapshot the home takes as the truth about that host. A worker's secrets are the node's `env` file — a fine-grained PAT scoped to contractmatch as `GH_TOKEN` (without it the first grinder pushes a branch and cannot open its PR, since the image's `gh` has no `~/.config/gh` and `git push` reads `.netrc`, which `gh` does not), the author name and email, a Doppler service token — never the person's.

   **Step 3c, in order — each its own PR where it stands alone:**
   1. **The per-node link socket.** Home: one listener per `nodes:` entry at `~/.agentorc/links/<name>/link.sock`, read at start (a new node is a restart), entering `_serve_link` with the configured name. Node: `link: {socket: <path>}` in `hosts.py` and `link._dial_once`. Test (`pdm run test`): a node in its own process with its own `AGENTORC_HOME` dials the socket with no ssh, comes up, survives a restart of the home (the directory, not the file, is what it holds).
   2. **`sessionorc/containers.py` and `ao host up|rebuild|forget|status`.** Generate the node's definitions from the repo's — the base (`devcontainer build`, tagged) and the node on a generated Dockerfile stage that installs tmux at build (a local *feature* was the plan; the CLI takes one only from the repo's own `.devcontainer/`, seen live 2026-09-17); `forget` removes the container, the link directory and the `nodes:` entry and keeps the node's volume unless `--purge`; `devcontainer up` under agentorc's id label; provision — refuse naming what is missing (Python 3.12+ in the image, the devcontainer CLI on the home), venv, the wheel, the node's `hosts.yml`, `gh auth setup-git`; start the agent (`docker exec -d`, a pidfile and a log under the node's `/agentorc/home`, `init: true` in the definition); `reach` derived from the entry. **Includes the promote's new step**: `ao service install` (TD-062, CLAUDE.md's promote pair) writes the wheel of what it installed to `~/.agentorc/wheels/` — there is no wheel to provision from until it does, so it is in this PR, not assumed. Tests: the generator in `pdm run test`, contractmatch's `devcontainer.json` as a fixture — mounts dropped and stood in, paths re-anchored, the two mounts, the Dockerfile stage and `init` added, `workspaceMount` set; `up` and provisioning driven through a subprocess seam with a fake `devcontainer`/`docker` recording the calls. **Live check, on kmaster, not in the suite** (docker is not in `pdm run test`): `ao host up contractmatch` from nothing to a linked card, the person's VS Code container untouched beside it.
   3. **The supervisor** in the home's tick — container gone / agent gone (no live pid behind the pidfile) / protocol refused — with backoff and the card's overlay naming which; a stopped container reads as a reboot. Test (`pdm run test`): the decision as a function over (container state, pid state, link state, last attempt) with the fake from 3c.2; live: `docker kill` the agent inside and watch the card come back.
   4. **Occupancy across the home and its container nodes** at `create`, derived from `container:`. Test (`pdm run test`): records on two hosts over one `dir`, one host a `container:` node of the other, and `occupants()` at the home naming both; live: `ao new` in the container's checkout while a kmaster session holds it is refused, and the reverse.
   5. **Reach for the terminal and the VS Code link** via the `container:` entry — with, or folded into, step 4's remote terminal.
   **The person's steps**, once: the `nodes:` entry; `npm i -g @devcontainers/cli` on kmaster (done 2026-09-17, user-level, `~/.npm-global/bin`); the repo's `.devcontainer` re-pointed and its user pinned (above; **confirmed live 2026-09-17**: `setup_scripts/postCreate.sh` dies on `mkdir /workspaces/contractmatch/.claude`, and `useradd` without `--uid` gave `developer` uid 1000 by luck) — **not tmux**: contractmatch's image has none, and Sonnet round 3 put it on this list, but the home installs the client, so it installs tmux, by a generated Dockerfile stage on the repo's image (Paul, 2026-09-17); the node's `env` file; one login inside for the `grind` profile; and, after step 4, `contractmatch: ~/contractmatch` beside `kmaster:` in the project's repo entry and `host: contractmatch` on `cm-grind`.

   **Sequencing.** 3c.1 alone proves the link with a container run by hand. 3c.2–3 make it a host; the first grinder is `ao new --unattended` run *inside* it, watched from kmaster as a read-only card, with its wrap-up run by the node's own stop policy — `create` is gated at the home, and acts, mail and cross-host `ao team start` are steps 4–5 — and `ao team start cm-grind` from kmaster is the done-when. Prove it on contractmatch before guardians (§10, 2026-09-13), which is the same node over ssh.

4. **Reports and acts across the link**: nodes report state, hook events and pane evidence, and on reconnect send a full snapshot first and then their bounded spool, the home dropping events older than the snapshot (*decided in 4b.2: no spool — the snapshot of records is the whole of it, because nothing at the home consumes an event; design §4.4a*); merges by field owner; unknown reported records adopted; the home pushes each node its records' policy fields and the node runs the **stopping** policies locally, including while the link is down, while starting policies run at the home; permission prompts waiting on the node, answered over the link, *host unreachable* when it drops; reachability as the home's per-host link state; a person's create on an offline node served locally and adopted later; `ao` and `ao ui` on a node falling back to that host's sessions, labelled *offline*, while home is unreachable; cross-host `ao team start` — `host:` on a team definition in `org.yml` (default: the host the start runs on), `teams.plan` resolving each repo's checkout on that host and creating there, refused while that host is down; a nightly tarball of the home's store; acts gated at the home and executed by the node, refused when the host is unreachable; a node's sessions refused mail and acts while its link is down, a person at that host unaffected; the UI and the CLI reading the org from the home. **Split 2026-09-17: 4a** (landed, the status line above) is the `act` method, the one-graph gate, `create` with `host`, `host:` on a team and the per-member stop; **4b** (brief: `docs/briefs/td057-step4b.md`, run by the worker `td057-step4b` and removed with PR #224, written 2026-09-18 after step 5 and PR #219 — three PRs: pane reads and a permission over the link; the home's intent pushed to the replica with the unread count, the node's derived reports and offline arrivals, the spool decided; a team on a machine node, the nightly tarball, the node's own UI; step 5 already built *`ao` on a node forwarding to the home*) holds the policy split — the home pushing every home-owned field to the node on reconnect and the node's replica running the stopping policies from it — permission prompts waiting on a node and answered over the link, a person's offline `create` adopted later, `ao` and `ao ui` on a node forwarding to the home (the caller then reaches the home's gate as `id@node`, which the graph is already keyed for), the spool of hook events, a link method that reads a checkout's roles and briefs on the node, and the nightly tarball. **Step 4 is complete (4b.1–4b.3, PRs #222–#224, 2026-09-18)**; what is left of TD-057 is its *Done when*, checked on Paul's machines.
5. **Mail across hosts** (after TD-052 steps 1–3): *reachable* includes the link being up; the doorbell as a `ring(id, count)` act to the node; `wait` forwarded from a node with cancellation when the CLI's connection closes; mail to an unreachable host landing at home with *landed — host unreachable* on the sender's reply and card; the person inbox per org. Test on Paul's machines: a laptop grinder mails its kmaster lead, the laptop sleeps mid-thread, the lead's reply shows *landed — host unreachable*, the laptop wakes and the grinder is rung once. **Landed (status line above): the forwarded `wait` is the doorbell, so no `ring` act was needed; the laptop test is the live check.**

**Later, not in scope:** spooled sends from an offline host with the gate's verdict returned on reconnect; the terminal websocket and attachments over the link (§4.5b needs it for the relay); a hosted relay as an external home.

**Done when** a session on the laptop and its lead on kmaster exchange mail and the lead's acts reach it, every gate reads one graph at the home, a sleeping laptop loses nothing and is woken once on return, and phase 1 (one host, no `home:`) behaves exactly as before.

**Related:** design §4.4a (the whole section), §4.5b, §4.6, §4.8, §4.10, §5, §7, §9 invariants 1, 2 (the container hole), 11, 12, 13, 15, §10 (the 2026-09-16 entry, and the 2026-09-13 devcontainer entry with its 2026-09-17 follow-up); TD-052 (mail on one host, whose step 1 carries this entry's step 1 choices), TD-004 (host identity), ADR 2026-09-16 (messaging prior art).

## TD-058: Stopping the host agent hangs until systemd kills it

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-16 (observed by the anchor session restarting the units for TD-052's live check)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** live-check
**Pickable:** no — what is left is the live stop of both units

**Status:** Partly done — **the agent half is fixed 2026-09-17 (PR #176)**: `serve()` no longer awaits `serve_forever()`, whose own cancel path awaits `wait_closed()` before any `finally` of ours can run (closing connections in a `finally` around it still hangs — tried first); it tracks every client connection in `_handle_conn` (`_conns`) and on cancel closes the server and each connection before `async with server` waits, so a blocked `wait` sees the close, is cancelled and writes its cursor. `test_stop_closes_a_subscriber_and_a_blocked_wait` stops an agent holding a subscriber and a 600 s `wait` within a second (unfixed, it hangs). **The UI half is fixed too (PR #177)**: the `/events` handler sat in its agent subscription until the *next* event's send failed, since the page never sends on that socket — and uvicorn's shutdown closes each websocket's transport and then waits for its handler, so the UI stopped whenever something next changed (the 40 s). The handler now races the stream against a watch for the websocket's disconnect and ends on whichever comes first; `test_ui_stop.py` drives the ASGI app against an idle agent and asserts the handler ends, and its agent subscription goes, within 2 s of the disconnect (unfixed, it does not — the TestClient cannot show this, since it cancels a websocket handler on exit). Established by that test, not yet on the live unit. `/term/` needed nothing: its pump reads the disconnect. **Remaining:** (1) the live check in **Done when** below, now for both units (docs/user_attention.md); (2) the `TimeoutStopSec` backstop, not added — consider it only if the live check still shows a slow stop. History — **cause confirmed 2026-09-16 22:36 MDT**: with `agentorc-ui` stopped first (closing its `subscribe` connection) and no `wait` blocked, `systemctl --user stop agentorc-agent` returned in under a second and the journal shows a clean `Stopped`, no timeout and no SIGKILL. The same run found a second, separate hang: `systemctl --user stop agentorc-ui` took 40 s to return, so the UI has a slow stop of its own (open terminal websockets or the agent subscription held through uvicorn's graceful shutdown are the likely holders — not diagnosed). Until this is fixed, stop the UI before the agent **Verified live 2026-09-17 00:41:** with #176 and #177 loaded, `systemctl --user restart agentorc-ui agentorc-agent` returned in under a second with six sessions attached; the restart before it (00:07, old code) took the full 90 s.

**Location:** `src/agentorc/ui/app.py` (the UI's shutdown), `src/sessionorc/agent.py` (`HostAgent.serve`: `async with server: await server.serve_forever()`; `serve_until_signal`), `src/agentorc/service.py` (the unit: `KillMode=process`, no `TimeoutStopSec`)

**Why:** on 2026-09-16 21:24 MDT `systemctl --user restart agentorc-agent` sat in `stop-sigterm` for 90 s, then systemd logged `State 'stop-sigterm' timed out. Killing.` and sent SIGKILL (`journalctl --user -u agentorc-agent`). SIGTERM cancels the serve task (TD-024), and leaving `async with server` calls `Server.wait_closed()`, which since Python 3.12 waits for **every open client connection** to finish — and the UI's `subscribe` connection never does, nor does a CLI blocked in the `wait` RPC (TD-052 step 3, which makes long-lived connections the normal case for every lead). So a stop never completes on its own. Nothing is lost today — tmux holds the sessions, the store is written on every change, and the unit restarts — but the socket file is not unlinked by the `finally`, in-flight permission waiters die without an answer, every restart costs 90 s during which hooks queue to disk, and a SIGKILL is the wrong default for a process that will soon hold the org's mail.

**Fix:** on shutdown, close the client connections the agent holds (subscribers, `_waits`, and any other open writer — track them in `_handle_conn`) before awaiting `wait_closed()`, or bound the wait with a short timeout and then close them; cancel blocked `wait` RPCs so each writes its cursor; keep the `finally` that unlinks the socket reachable. A test: an agent with one subscriber and one blocked `wait` stops within a second of the serve task being cancelled. Consider `TimeoutStopSec=10` on the unit as the backstop, not the fix.

**Done when** `systemctl --user restart agentorc-agent` returns in a few seconds with the UI open and a lead blocked in `ao wait`, and the journal shows a clean stop.

**Related:** TD-024 (archived: the pending-task traceback on SIGTERM, which made the cancel path clean but did not meet this), TD-052 step 3 (the `wait` RPC's long-lived connections), design §4.4, §4.6.

## TD-060: The name `agentorc` is taken — rename the project

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-16 (Paul; discussed in samscrape session 746e1973 on kmaster)
**Owner:** paul
**Kind:** decision
**Pickable:** no — step 2 waits for the release boundary Paul picks

**Status:** Open — **decided by Paul 2026-09-22: go with the rename to `shiftlead`** (his words: *go with the rename to shiftlead, we will have to figure out when to rename the repo*). **Step 1 is done:** its remaining half, the visible name — the UI wordmark in `base.html` (*Shift**Lead***), every page's `<title>` (*Org · ShiftLead*) and the mockups' wordmark — landed 2026-09-22 (grinder-ao-2). **Step 2, the machine names** (`~/.agentorc`, the seven env vars, the units, the venv, `.agentorc.yml`, the `agentorc-hook` entry point, with the migration and its test), waits for a release boundary with no team running and both units stopped, as *Fix* says, and the anchor runs it. **When the GitHub repo is renamed is Paul's, and open** (board line, 2026-09-22): it can follow step 2 or go first, since GitHub redirects the old name. Was: awaiting Paul's decision on the name. `shiftlead` is the leading candidate (free on PyPI, npm, crates.io and as a GitHub org when checked 2026-09-16; `.dev`/`.org`/`.tech` open per Paul); Paul asked for time to think. Nothing is renamed. **Both names were claimed 2026-09-17 ahead of the decision:** PyPI `shiftlead 0.0.0` (empty placeholder) and the GitHub org `ShiftLead`; the domains are not registered. The candidates, the test they were held to, the measured surface and the six decisions are in [ADR 2026-09-16](decisions/2026-09-16-rename.md). Worktree `agentorc_rename` exists with no commits.

**Location:** the whole repo — 1,110 case-insensitive occurrences of `agentorc` in 93 tracked files and 260 of `sessionorc` in 56 (`origin/main` at `8bd21b9`) — plus what lives outside it: `~/.agentorc/`, seven `AGENTORC_*` environment variables (the six below plus `AGENTORC_BUILD`, added since this was measured), `agentorc-agent.service` / `agentorc-ui.service`, `~/.local/share/agentorc-venv`, `.agentorc.yml` in other repos, the `ao-` session-id prefix, and three synced files in dev-cadence (and so in samscrape and contractmatch).

**Why:** another project holds the name (TD-059 is to identify it). This is the second rename (`sessionherd` → `agentorc`, 2026-09-04, chosen to sit beside `cmdorc`), and it is cheaper now than it will ever be again: one user, nothing published to PyPI, no external adapter known. Every week adds briefs, ADRs and session records that carry the old name.

**Fix:** decide the six questions in the ADR (the name; what happens to `sessionorc`; whether the `ao-` id prefix stays as an opaque historical prefix — recommended; whether `ao` survives as an alias; a compatibility window or a clean cut with a `migrate` command; whether to claim the names before deciding). Then, in order: claim PyPI + the GitHub org; land TD-055 first, since it edits many of the same lines; one PR for packages, entry points, env vars, state dir, units and config file name, with the migration and a test for it; the text sweep, **leaving dated records — ADRs, the ledger archive, memories — as written**; dev-cadence's three synced files and a sync; the repo rename and a README tagline drawn from TD-059. **Land the machine-facing step with no team running and both units stopped:** running sessions hold `AGENTORC_SESSION` in their environment and resolve `agentorc-hook` from the unit's PATH, so a rename under a live team breaks every started session's hooks.

**See TD-076 before deciding (2026-09-20):** on 2026-09-19 Paul decided the coordinating role becomes `manager`, the go-between `techlead`, and bare `lead` is retired — which weakens the *shift lead is the job* argument for `shiftlead` and should be weighed in the same sitting.

**2026-09-21, Paul: the spelling is `ShiftLead`, `shiftlead.dev` is bought (Cloudflare), and the command stays `ao`** — short, in every brief and every worker's habit, and `sl` is a joke command on many Linux boxes; `shiftlead` may be a long-form alias. **The order confirmed by Paul 2026-09-21** (*that order works*) — **step 1 begun the same evening: the README's and the design's titles and the glossary say ShiftLead (this PR); the wordmark in `base.html` and the page `<title>` are a worker's, one line each; step 2 waits for a release boundary.** As suggested: **the visible name first** (the UI wordmark, the README, the design's title and this ledger's head — one PR, cheap, the page half a worker's) and **the machine names at a release boundary** (`~/.agentorc`, the seven env vars, the units, the venv, `.agentorc.yml`, with the migration and its test) with no team running, as *Fix* says. **Decided by Paul, 2026-09-20: the project keeps `shiftlead`** — with the roles `manager` and `techlead` (TD-076) — so the name stands for what the product is for, not for a role in it; the ADR's *the metaphor is the job* paragraph is to be reworded when the rename is carried out. What remains of this entry is the carrying out.

**Done when** the installed distribution, both units, the state directory and the README carry the new name; a session started before the rename is either migrated or documented as needing a restart; `git grep -i agentorc` returns only dated records and the "was `agentorc`" lines; and the three dev-cadence files are synced.

**Related:** TD-059 (the neighbours the name search found; its survey supplies the tagline), TD-055 (the glossary rename — `lead` is already the decided word, which is much of why `shiftlead` fits), design §4.1 (session ids), §4.3 (the two packages). **README positioning (TD-059, 2026-09-20):** the first-screen sentence drawn from the neighbours survey is in [ADR 2026-09-20](decisions/2026-09-20-session-desk-neighbours.md) (c); it goes in with the rename.

## TD-061: A worker's memory write lands uncommitted in the anchor's checkout

**Priority:** Medium
**Added:** 2026-09-16 (observed by the anchor session; Paul asked for the entry). Numbered 061 because PR #173, open from another session, holds TD-059 and TD-060.
**Owner:** dev-cadence
**Kind:** decision
**Pickable:** no — the memory wiring is dev-cadence's SYNCED files

**Status:** Open

**Location:** `.claude/settings.local.json` (`autoMemoryDirectory`, an absolute path to the main checkout's `docs/claude-memory/`), `scripts/hydrate_worktree.sh` (symlinks that file into every worktree — a SYNCED FILE, dev-cadence's), `scripts/check_claude_memory.sh` (the guard — also synced), docs/cadence.md §1 and the memory section; design §4.2 (the launch layer agentorc's own sessions get)

**Why:** `autoMemoryDirectory` is `/home/kmaster/agentorc/docs/claude-memory`, and `hydrate_worktree.sh` symlinks `.claude/settings.local.json` into each worktree so a worker keeps the repo's memory path. The path is absolute, so a session working in `.claude/worktrees/<name>` writes its memory files into the **main checkout's** working tree, not its own worktree's. The write is on no branch of the worker's, cannot ride the worker's PR, and sits uncommitted in the anchor's tree until somebody commits there. On 2026-09-16 `tdgrind-ao-1` added a wrap-up lesson to `docs/claude-memory/agentorc-td-grind-mechanics.md` this way; the anchor session's next `git commit -a`, for an unrelated ledger change, swept it into PR #174, where the Sonnet fact-check noticed a file the PR did not describe. It was kept and explained in the PR body. The failure has three faces: a worker's lesson is stranded if the anchor never commits (cadence's *never strand work*, applied to memory); it lands in a PR whose author did not write it and whose review did not cover it; and it breaks the one-agent-per-checkout rule (§9 invariant 2) in spirit — a second session is editing the anchor's tree. `check_claude_memory.sh` does see part of it: its uncommitted-memory check runs `git status` on the memory directory and warns *N uncommitted change(s) … commit & push*, at the next SessionStart of any session in the repo. What it cannot say is whose write it was, and its advice — commit it — is exactly how the file ends up in the wrong session's PR.

**Fix:** decide where a worktree session's memory belongs, in dev-cadence, since the wiring is its synced files: either the memory path resolves **per checkout** (a relative `autoMemoryDirectory`, or the hydrate step writing the worktree's own `docs/claude-memory` path instead of symlinking the anchor's settings), so a worker's memory write is a change on its own branch and rides its own PR; or memory stays shared and the wrap-up rule in every brief becomes *memory changes go through a PR of their own from the session that wrote them*, with `check_claude_memory.sh`'s existing uncommitted-memory warning naming the file and saying that a session which did not write it should leave it for the one that did.
